\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}=\Sigma\cup\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Goes\_wrong}}}\}
, and
a target alphabet
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\Sigma_{T}}}}=\Sigma\cup\Sigma^{\prime}
.
The trace relation is defined in the same way and we obtain the same property
mappings as above, except that since target traces now have more events (some of
which may be dangerous), and the arbitrary continuations of target traces get
more interesting.
For instance, consider a new event that represents writing data on the hard
drive, and suppose we want to prove that this event cannot happen for a
compiled program.
Then, proving this property requires exactly proving that the source
program exhibits no undefined behavior
[
11
]
.
More generally,
what one can prove about target-only events can only be either that they
cannot appear (because there is no undefined behavior) or that any of them can
appear (in the case of undefined behavior).

In
§ 5.2
we study a similar example, showing that even
in a safe language linked adversarial contexts can cause dangerous target events
that have no source correspondent.

3.2
Resource Exhaustion

Let us return to the discussion about resource exhaustion in

§ 1
.

Example 2
(Resource Exhaustion)

We consider traces made of events drawn from
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}
in the source, and

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\Sigma_{T}}}}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}\cup\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Resource\_Limit\_Hit}}}\}
in the target.
Recall the trace relation for resource exhaustion:

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\quad\equiv\quad\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\vee\exists{m}\leq\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}.~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}={m}\cdot\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Resource\_Limit\_Hit}}}.

Formally, this relation is similar to the one for undefined behavior, except
this time it is the target trace that is allowed to end early instead of the
source trace.

The induced trace property
mappings
\tilde{\sigma}
and
\tilde{\tau}
are the following
(

):

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{T}}}})

\displaystyle=\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{T}}}}\}\cap\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\mid\forall{m}\leq\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}.~{}{m}\cdot\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Resource\_Limit\_Hit}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{T}}}}\};

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\cup\{{m}\cdot\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Resource\_Limit\_Hit}}}\mid\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}.~{}{m}\leq\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\}.

These capture the following intuitions.
The target-to-source mapping
\tilde{\sigma}
states that
to prove a property of the compiled program
one has to show that the traces of the source program
satisfy two conditions: (1) they must also satisfy the target property; and
(2) the termination of every one of their prefixes by a resource
exhaustion error must be allowed by the target property.
This is rather restrictive:
any property that prevents resource exhaustion cannot be proved
using source-level reasoning. Indeed, if
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}
does not allow
resource exhaustion, then
\tilde{\sigma}(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\varnothing}}}
.
This is to be expected since resource exhaustion is simply not accounted for
at the source level.
The other mapping
\tilde{\tau}
states that a compiled program produces traces that
either belong to the same properties as the traces of the source program or
end early due to resource exhaustion.

In this example, safety properties
[
32
]
are mapped
(in both directions) to other safety properties (

).
This can be desirable for a relation: since safety properties are usually easier
to reason about, one interested only in safety properties at the target can
reason about them using source-level reasoning tools for safety properties.

§ 0.B.1
provides a detailed account of safety
properties and how one can restrict the notion of compiler correctness if one
is only interested in them.

The compiler correctness theorem in CakeML is an instance of
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
for the
\sim
relation above.
We have also implemented two small compilers that are correct for this relation.
The full details can be found in the Coq development in the supplementary
materials. The first compiler (

) goes from a simple
expression language (similar to the one in
§ 3.3
but
without inputs)
to the same language except that execution is bounded by some amount of fuel: each execution
step consumes some amount of fuel and execution immediately halts when it
runs out of fuel. The compiler is the identity.

The second compiler (

) is
more interesting: we proved this
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
instance
for a variant of a compiler from a

while
language to a simple stack machine by Xavier Leroy
[
36
]
.
We enriched the two languages with outputs and modified the semantics of the stack machine so that
it falls into an error state if the stack reaches a certain size.
The proof uses a standard
forward simulation modified to account for
failure.

\boxdot

We conclude this subsection by noting that the resource exhaustion relation and
the undefined behavior relation from the previous subsection can easily be combined.
Indeed, given a relation
\sim_{\text{UB}}
and a
relation
\sim_{\text{RE}}
defined as above on the same sets of traces,
we can build a new relation
\sim
that allows both refinement of undefined
behavior and resource exhaustion by taking their union:

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\equiv\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim_{\text{UB}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\vee\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim_{\text{RE}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
.
A compiler that is
\mathsf{CC^{\sim_{\text{UB}}}}
or
\mathsf{CC^{\sim_{\text{RE}}}}
is
trivially
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
, though the converse is not true.

3.3
Different Source and Target Values

We now illustrate trace-relating compilation for a translation
mapping source-level booleans to target-level natural numbers. Given
the simplicity of this compiler, most of the details of the
formalization are deferred to the online appendix.

The source language is a pure, statically typed expression language whose
expressions
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e}}}
include naturals
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}
, booleans
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{b}}}
, conditionals,
arithmetic and relational operations, boolean inputs
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{in_{b}}}}
and natural
inputs
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{in_{n}}}}
.
A trace
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}
is a list of inputs
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{is}}}
paired with a
result
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{r}}}
, which can be a natural, a boolean, or an
error. Well-typed programs never produce error (

).
Types
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{ty}}}
are either
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{N}}}
(naturals) or
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{B}}}

(booleans); typing is standard.
The source language has a standard big-step operational semantics
(
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e\bm{\rightsquigarrow}\left\langle is,r\right\rangle}}}
) which tells how an expression
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e}}}
generates a
trace
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle is,r\right\rangle}}}
.
The target language is analogous, except that it is untyped, only
has naturals
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}
and its only inputs are naturals
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{in_{n}}}}
.
The semantics of the target language is also given in big-step style. Since
we only have naturals and all expressions operate on them, no error
result is possible in the target.

The compiler is homomorphic, translating a source expression to the same
target expression; the only differences are natural numbers (and conditionals),
as noted below.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{true}}}\right\downarrow}}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{1}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{in_{b}}}}\right\downarrow}}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{in_{n}}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e_{1}\leq e_{2}}}}\right\downarrow}}=

\displaystyle\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{if}~{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e_{1}}}}\right\downarrow}}~{}\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\leq}}}~{}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e_{2}}}}\right\downarrow}}~{}{then}~{}1~{}{else}~{}0}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{false}}}\right\downarrow}}=

Relating Traces.

We relate basic values (naturals and booleans) in a non-injective fashion as
noted below. Then, we extend the relation to lists of inputs pointwise
(
Sections

3.3
and
3.3
) and lift that relation to traces
(
Sections

3.3
and
3.3
).

Property mappings.

The property mappings

\tilde{\sigma}
and
\tilde{\tau}
induced by the trace relation
\sim
defined
above capture the intuition behind encoding booleans as naturals:

•

the source-to-target mapping allows
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{true}}}
to be
encoded by any non-zero number;

•

the target-to-source mapping requires that

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{0}}}
be replaceable by
both

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{0}}}
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{false}}}
.

Compiler correctness.

With the relation above, the compiler is proven to satisfy
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.

Theorem 3.1
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\cdot}}}\right\downarrow}}
is correct

)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\cdot}}}\right\downarrow}}
is
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.

Simulations with different traces.

The difficulty in proving
Theorem 3.1
arises from the
trace-relating compilation setting:
For compilation chains that have the same source and target traces, it is
customary to prove compiler correctness using a forward
simulation (i.e., a simulation between source and target transition system);
then, using determinacy
[
18
,
40
]
of the target language
and input totality
[
69
,
19
]
(aka receptiveness)
of the source, this forward simulation is flipped
into a backward simulation (a simulation between target and source transition
system), as described by
Beringer et al.
[
7
],
Leroy
[
35
]
.
This flipping is useful because forward simulations are often much easier to
prove (by induction on the transitions of the source) than backward ones,
as it is the case here.

We first give the main idea of the flipping proof, when the inputs are the same
in the source and the target
[
7
,
35
]
.
We only consider inputs, as it is the most interesting case, since
with determinacy, nondeterminism only occurs on inputs.
Given a forward simulation
\mathcal{R}
, and a target program
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
that simulates
a source program
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}
,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
is able to perform an input iff
so is
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}
: otherwise, say for instance that
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}
performs an output,
by forward simulation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
would also perform an output, which is
impossible because of determinacy. By input totality of the source,

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}
must be able to perform the exact same input as
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
;
using forward simulation and determinacy, the resulting programs
must be related.

\textstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{i_{1}}}}}
\textstyle{=\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{i_{2}}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{i_{1}}}}}

By input totality

By contradiction,

using forward simulation

and determinacy

\textstyle{\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{W_{S}}_{1}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}

By forward simulation and determinacy

\textstyle{\cdot}
\textstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{W_{T}}_{1}}}}}

However, our trace relation is not injective
(both
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{0}}}
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{false}}}
are
mapped to
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{0}}}
), therefore these arguments do
not apply: not all possible inputs of target programs are
accounted for in the forward simulation.
We thus have to strengthen the forward simulation assumption,
requiring the following additional property to hold, for any source program
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}

and target program
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
related by the forward simulation
\mathcal{R}
.

\textstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{1}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{2}}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{1}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{2}}}}}
\textstyle{\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{W_{S}}_{2}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{R}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{W_{S}}_{1}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{W_{T}}_{1}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{W_{T}}_{2}}}}}

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{1}}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{1}}}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{1}}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{2}}}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{2}}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{2}}}}

We say that a forward simulation for which this property
holds is
flippable
.
For our example compiler, a flippable forward simulation works as follows:
whenever a boolean input occurs in the source, the target
program must perform every strictly positive input
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}
(and not just
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{1}}}
,
as suggested by the compiler).
Using this property, determinacy of the target, input totality of the
source, as well as the fact that any target input has an inverse image
through the relation, we can indeed show that the forward
simulation can be turned into a backward one: starting from

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W_{S}}}}\mathrel{\mathcal{R}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{W_{T}}}}
and an input
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{2}}}}
, we show that
there is
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{1}}}}
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{i_{T}}_{2}}}}
as in the
diagram above, using the same arguments as when the inputs are the same;
because the simulation is flippable, we can close the diagram, and obtain
the existence of an adequate
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{i_{S}}_{2}}}}
. From this we obtain
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.

In fact, we have proven a completely general ‘flipping theorem’,
with this flippable hypothesis on the forward simulation
(

).
We have also shown that if the relation
\sim
defines a bijection between the
inputs of the source and the target, then any forward simulation is flippable,
hence reobtaining the usual proof technique
[
7
,
35
]
as a
special case.
This flipping theorem is further discussed in the online appendix.

3.4
Abstraction Mismatches

We now consider how to relate traces where a single source action is compiled to
multiple target ones.
To illustrate this, we take a pure, statically-typed source language that can
output (nested) pairs of arbitrary size, and a pure,
untyped
target language
where sent values have a fixed size.
Concretely, the source is analogous to the language
of
§ 3.3
, except that it does not have inputs or
booleans and it has an expression
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{send~{}e}}}
,
which can emit a (nested) pair
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e}}}
of
values in a single action. That is, given that
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e}}}
reduces to a pair,
e.g.,
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle v1,\left\langle v2,v3\right\rangle\right\rangle}}}
,
expression
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{send~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle v1,\left\langle v2,v3\right\rangle\right\rangle}}}}}}
emits
action
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle v1,\left\langle v2,v3\right\rangle\right\rangle}}}
.
That expression is compiled into a sequence of individual sends in the target
language
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}v1}}}

;

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}v2}}}

;

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}v3}}}
, since
in the target,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}e}}}
sends the value that
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{e}}}
reduces to, but
the language has no pairs.

Due to space constraints we omit the full formalization of these simple languages
and of the homomorphic compiler (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left(\cdot\right)}}}\right\downarrow}}:\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e}}}\to\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{e}}}
).
The only interesting bit is the compilation of the
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{send~{}\cdot}}}
expression, which
relies on the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{gensend}\left(\cdot\right)}}}
function below.
That function takes a source expression of a given type and returns a sequence
of target
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}\cdot}}}
instructions that send each element of the expression.

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{gensend}\left(\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e:\tau}}}\right)}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{send~{}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left(\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{e}}}}:N\right)}}}\right\downarrow}}}}}}}}}&\text{ if }\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\tau}}}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{N}}}\\
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{gensend}\left(\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e.1:\tau^{\prime}}}}\right)}}};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{gensend}\left(\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{e.2:\tau^{\prime\prime}}}}\right)}}}}}}&\text{ if }\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\tau}}}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\tau^{\prime}\times\tau^{\prime\prime}}}}\end{cases}

Relating Traces.

We start with the trivial relation between numbers:
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}\operatorname{\sim^{0}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}
, i.e., numbers are related when they are the same.
We cannot build a relation between single actions since a single source action is
related to multiple target ones. Therefore, we define a relation between a source
action
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}
and a target trace
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
(a list of numbers),
inductively on the structure
of
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}
(which is a pair of values, and values are natural numbers or pairs).

\begin{array}[]{c}\textsf{\scriptsize({Trace-Rel-N-N})}\\
\inference{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}\operatorname{\sim^{0}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}&\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n^{\prime}}}}\operatorname{\sim^{0}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n^{\prime}}}}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle n,n^{\prime}\right\rangle}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n\cdot n^{\prime}}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}\textsf{\scriptsize({Trace-Rel-N-M})}\\
\inference{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}\operatorname{\sim^{0}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}&\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle n,M\right\rangle}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n\cdot t}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}\textsf{\scriptsize({Trace-Rel-M-N})}\\
\inference{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}&\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}\operatorname{\sim^{0}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle M,n\right\rangle}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t\cdot n}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}\textsf{\scriptsize({Trace-Rel-M-M})}\\
\inference{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}&\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M^{\prime}}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t^{\prime}}}}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left\langle M,M^{\prime}\right\rangle}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t\cdot t^{\prime}}}}}\end{array}

A pair of naturals is related to the two actions that send each element of the
pair (
Section

3.4
).
If a pair is made of sub-pairs, we require all such sub-pairs to be related
(
Sections

3.4
,
3.4
and
3.4
).
We build on these rules to define the

\begin{array}[]{c}\textsf{\scriptsize({Trace-Rel-Single})}\\
\inference{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}&\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t^{\prime}}}}}{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s\cdot M}}}\operatorname{\sim}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t\cdot t^{\prime}}}}}\end{array}

a

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
relation between source and target traces
for which the compiler is correct (
Theorem 3.2
).
Trivially, traces are related when they are both empty. Alternatively, given related traces, we can concatenate
a source action and a second target trace provided that they are related
(

3.4
).

Theorem 3.2
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left(\cdot\right)}}}\right\downarrow}}
is correct)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\left(\cdot\right)}}}\right\downarrow}}
is
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.

With our trace relation, the trace property mappings capture the following
intuitions:

•

The target-to-source mapping states that a source property can
reconstruct target action as it sees fit. For example, trace

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{{4}\cdot{6}\cdot{5}\cdot{7}}}}
is related to

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{\left\langle 4,6\right\rangle}\cdot{\left\langle 5,7\right\rangle}}}}
and

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{\left\langle\left\langle 4,\left\langle 6,\left\langle 5,7\right\rangle\right\rangle\right\rangle\right\rangle}}}}
(and many more variations).
This gives freedom to the source implementation of a target behavior, which
follows from the non-injectivity of
\sim
.
5
5
5
Making
\sim
injective is a matter of adding open and close parenthesis
actions in target traces.

•

The source-to-target mapping “forgets” about the way pairs
are nested, but is faithful w.r.t. the values
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{v_{i}}}}

contained in a message.
Notice that source safety properties are always mapped to
target safety properties. For instance, if
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Safety_{S}}}}

prescribes that some bad number is never sent, then

\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})
prescribes the same number
is never sent in the target and
\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})\in\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Safety_{T}}}}
.
Of course if

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Safety_{S}}}}
prescribes that a particular nested pairing
like
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{\left\langle 4,\left\langle 6,\left\langle 5,7\right\rangle\right\rangle\right\rangle}}}}
never
happens, then
\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})
is still a target safety
property, but the trivial one, since

\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\top}}}\in\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Safety_{T}}}}
.

4
Trace-Relating Compilation and Noninterference Preservation

When source and target observations are drawn from the same set, a
correct compiler (
\mathsf{CC^{=}}
) is enough to ensure the preservation of
all subset-closed
hyperproperties, in particular of

noninterference
(NI)
[
22
]
,
as also mentioned at the beginning of
§ 2.3
.
In the scenario where target observations are strictly more informative
than source observations, the best guarantee one may expect from a correct trace-relating compiler
(
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
) is a
weakening
(or
declassification
) of target
noninterference that matches the noninterference property satisfied in the source.
To formalize this reasoning, this section applies the trinitarian view of
trace-relating compilation to the general framework of abstract noninterference
(ANI)
[
21
]
.

We first define NI and explain the issue of preserving source NI via a

\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
compiler. We then introduce ANI, which allows
characterizations of various forms of noninterference, and formulate a
general theory of ANI preservation via
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
. We also study how to
deal with cases such as undefined behavior in the target. Finally, we
answer the dual question, i.e., which source NI should be satisfied to
guarantee that compiled programs are noninterfering with respect to
target observers.

Intuitively, NI requires that publicly observable outputs do not
reveal information about private inputs. To define this formally, we
need a few additions to our setup. We indicate the (disjoint)

input
and
output
projections of a trace
t
as
t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}

and
t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}
respectively
6
6
6
Here we only require the projections
to be disjoint. Depending on the scenario and the attacker model the
projections might record information such as the ordering of
events.
.
Denote with
[t]_{\mathit{low}}
the equivalence class of a trace
t
,
obtained using a standard low-equivalence relation that relates low
(public) events only if they are equal, and ingores any difference
between private events.
Then, NI for source traces can be defined as:

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}=\left\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}.~{}[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}_{1}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}=[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}_{2}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}\Rightarrow[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}_{1}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}=[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}_{2}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}~{}\right\}.

That is, source NI comprises the sets of traces that have equivalent low output projections as long as their low input projections are equivalent.

Trace-Relating Compilation and Noninterference.

When additional observations are possible in the target, it is
unclear whether a noninterfering source program is compiled to a noninterfering
target program or not, and if so, whether the notion of NI in the
target is the expected or desired one.
We illustrate this issue considering a scenario where target traces
extend source ones by exposing the execution time.
While source noninterference
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}
requires
that private inputs do not affect public outputs,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{NI_{T}}}}

additionally requires that the execution time is not affected by
private inputs.

To model the scenario described, let
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Trace_{S}}}}
denote the set of
traces in the source, and

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Trace_{T}}}}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Trace_{S}}}}\times\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\omega}}}}}}}

be the set of target traces, where

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\omega}}}}}}}\triangleq\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N}\cup\{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\omega}}}\}}}}
.
Target traces have two components: a source trace, and a natural
number that denotes the time spent to produce the trace
(
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\omega}}}
if infinite).
Notice that if two source traces
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}}
, are
low-equivalent then
\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}}\}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}
and

\{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}})\}\in\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{NI_{T}}}}
, but

\{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{43}}})\}\not\in\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{NI_{T}}}}
and

\{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{43}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{43}}})\}\not\in\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{NI_{T}}}}
.

Consider the following straightforward trace relation, which relates a
source trace to any target trace whose first component is equal to it,
irrespective of execution time:

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\quad\equiv\quad\exists\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}.~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}=(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}).

A compiler is
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
if any trace that can be exhibited in the
target can be simulated in the source in some amount of time.
For such a compiler
Theorem 2.4
says that if
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}

satisfies
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\right\downarrow}}
satisfies

\mathit{Cl_{\subseteq}}\circ\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}})
, which however is
strictly weaker than
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{NI_{T}}}}
, as it contains, e.g.,
\{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{42}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{43}}}),(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{43}}})\}
, and one cannot conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\right\downarrow}}
is noninterfering in the target.
It is easy to prove that

\displaystyle\mathit{Cl_{\subseteq}}\circ\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{Cl_{\subseteq}}~{}(\left\{~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\times\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N^{\omega}}}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}\right\})=\left\{~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\times\mathcal{I}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}\wedge\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\omega}}}}}}}\right\},

the first equality coming from

\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\times\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbb{N^{\omega}}}}}
, and the second from
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}
being subset-closed.
As we will see, this hyperproperty
can
be characterized as a form of
NI, which one might call
timing-insensitive
noninterference
, and ensured only against attackers that cannot measure
execution time.
For this characterization, and to describe different forms of noninterference as well as formally analyze their preservation by a
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
compiler, we rely on the general framework of
abstract noninterference

[
21
]
.

Abstract Noninterference.

ANI
[
21
]

is a generalization of NI whose formulation relies on abstractions
(in abstract interpretation sense
[
16
]
) in order
to encompass arbitrary variants of NI. ANI is parameterized by an
observer
abstraction

\rho
, which denotes the distinguishing power of the attacker, and
a
selection abstraction

\phi
, which specifies when to check NI, and therefore captures a form of
declassification
[
58
]
.
7
7
7
ANI includes a third parameter
\eta
, which describes the maximal input variation that the
attacker may control. Here we omit
\eta
(i.e., take it to be the identity) in
order to simplify the presentation.
Formally:

\mathit{ANI}_{\phi}^{\rho}=\left\{\pi\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\forall t_{1}t_{2}\in\pi.~{}\phi(t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}_{1})=\phi(t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}_{2})\Rightarrow\rho(t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}_{1})=\rho(t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}_{2})\right\}.

By picking
\phi=\rho=[\cdot]_{\mathit{low}}
, we recover the standard noninterference
defined above, where NI must hold for all low inputs (i.e., no
declassification of private inputs), and the observational power of the attacker
is limited to distinguishing low outputs.

The observational power of the attacker can be weakened by
choosing a more liberal relation for
\rho
. For instance, one may limit the
attacker to observe the
parity
of output integer values. Another way to
weaken ANI is to use
\phi
to specify that noninterference is only required to
hold for a subset of low inputs.

To be formally precise,
\phi
and
\rho
are defined over sets of
(input and output projections of) traces, so when we write
\phi(t)
above, this
should be understood as a convenience notation for
\phi(\{t\})
. Likewise,

\phi=[\cdot]_{\mathit{low}}
should be understood as
\phi=\lambda\pi.\bigcup_{t\in\pi}[t]_{\mathit{low}}
, i.e., the powerset lifting of

[\cdot]_{\mathit{low}}
.
Additionally,
\phi
and
\rho
are required to be upper-closed operators
(
\mathit{uco}
)—i.e., monotonic, idempotent and extensive—on the poset that is the powerset of (input and output projections of) traces ordered by
inclusion
[
21
]
.

Trace-Relating Compilation and ANI for Timing.

We can now reformulate our example with observable execution times in the target
in terms of ANI. We have
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}=\mathit{ANI}_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho_{S}}}}}^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi_{S}}}}}
with

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi_{S}}}}=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho_{S}}}}=[\cdot]_{\mathit{low}}
. In this case, we can formally
describe the hyperproperty that a compiled program
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\right\downarrow}}
satisfies whenever

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}
satisfies
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}}
as an instance of ANI:

\displaystyle\mathit{Cl_{\subseteq}}\circ\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{NI_{S}}}})=\mathit{ANI}_{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}},

\displaystyle\text{ for }\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}=

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi_{S}}}}\text{ and }\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}})=\left\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},n)}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},n_{1})}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}.\;[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}=[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}_{1}}}}]_{\mathit{low}}\right\}.

The definition of
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}
tells us that the trace relation does not affect
the selection abstraction. The definition of
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}}
characterizes
