.
The mappings

\mathit{Safe}\circ\tilde{\tau}:2^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{{Trace}_{S}}}}}\leftrightarrows\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Safety_{T}}}}:\tilde{\sigma}

determine a Galois connection between source trace properties and
target safety properties, and ensure the equivalence

\mathsf{RTP^{\mathit{Safe}\circ\tilde{\tau}}}\iff\mathsf{RSP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}
(

).
This argument generalizes to arbitrary closure operators on target
properties (

) and on hyperproperties,
as long as the corresponding class is a sub-class of subset-closed
hyperproperties, and explains all but one of the asymmetries in

Figure 2
, the one that concerns the robust
preservation of arbitrary hyperproperties:

Theorem 5.3
(Weak Trinity for Robust
Hyperproperties

)

For a trace relation

{\sim}\subseteq\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{Trace_{S}}}}\times\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Trace_{T}}}}
and induced
property mappings
\tilde{\sigma}
and
\tilde{\tau}
,
\mathsf{RHC^{\sim}}

is equivalent to
\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\tau}}}
; moreover, if

\tilde{\tau}\leftrightarrows\tilde{\sigma}
is a Galois insertion
(i.e.,
\tilde{\tau}\circ\tilde{\sigma}=id
),
\mathsf{RHC^{\sim}}
implies

\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}
, while if

\tilde{\sigma}\leftrightarrows\tilde{\tau}
is a Galois
reflection (i.e.,
\tilde{\sigma}\circ\tilde{\tau}=id
),

\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}
implies
\mathsf{RHC^{\sim}}
,

\displaystyle\mbox{where }\mathsf{RHC^{\sim}}

\displaystyle\equiv\ \forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}~{}\forall\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{C_{T}}}}~{}\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{C_{S}}}}~{}\forall\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}.~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{C_{T}\left[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}\right\downarrow}}\right]}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\bm{\rightsquigarrow}}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\iff(\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{C_{S}\left[P\right]}}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\bm{\rightsquigarrow}}}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}});

\displaystyle\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\tau}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}~{}\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{H_{S}}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\models_{R}}}}~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{H_{S}}}}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}\right\downarrow}}~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\models}}}_{R}}}}~{}\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{H_{S}}}});

\displaystyle\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}

\displaystyle\equiv\forall\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}~{}\forall\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{H_{T}}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\models_{R}}}}~{}\tilde{\sigma}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{H_{T}}}})\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{P}}}\right\downarrow}}~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\models}}}_{R}}}}~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{H_{T}}}}.

This trinity is
weak
since extra hypotheses are needed to prove
some implications. While the equivalence
\mathsf{RHC^{\sim}}\iff\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\tau}}}

holds unconditionally, the other two implications hold only under
distinct, stronger assumptions.
For
\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}
it is still possible and correct to deduce a source
obligation for a given target hyperproperty
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{H_{T}}}}
when no
information is lost in the the composition

\tilde{\tau}\circ\tilde{\sigma}
(i.e., the two maps are a Galois

insertion
).
On the other hand,
\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\tau}}}
is a consequence of
\mathsf{RHP^{\tilde{\sigma}}}

when no information is lost in composing in the other direction,

\tilde{\sigma}\circ\tilde{\tau}
(i.e., the two maps are a Galois

reflection
).

Navigating the Diagram.

For a given trace relation
\sim
,

Figure 2
orders the generalized
criteria according to their relative strength. If a trinity implies
another (denoted by
\Rightarrow
), then the former provides stronger
security for a compilation chain than the latter.

As mentioned, some property-full criteria regarding proper subclasses
(i.e., subset-closed hyperproperties, safety, hypersafety,
2-relational safety and 2-relational hyperproperties) quantify over
arbitrary (relational) (hyper)properties and compose
\tilde{\tau}

with an additional operator.
We have already presented the
\mathit{Safe}
operator; other operators are

\mathit{Cl_{\subseteq}}
,
\mathit{HSafe}
, and
\mathit{2rSafe}
, which approximate the
image of
\tilde{\tau}
with a subset-closed hyperproperty, a
hypersafety and 2-relational safety respectively.

As a reading aid, when quantifying over arbitrary trace properties we
use the shaded blue as background color, we use the red when
quantifying over arbitrary subset-closed hyperproperties and green
for arbitrary 2-relational properties.

We now describe how to interpret the acronyms in

Figure

2
. All criteria start with
\mathsf{R}

meaning they refer to robust preservation. Criteria for relational
hyperproperties—here only arity 2 is shown—contain
\mathsf{2r}
.
Next, criteria names spell the class of hyperproperties they preserve:

\mathsf{H}
for hyperproperties,
\mathsf{SCH}
for subset-closed
hyperproperties,
\mathsf{HS}
for hypersafety,
\mathsf{T}
for trace
properties, and
\mathsf{S}
for safety properties. Finally,
property-free criteria end with a
\mathsf{C}
while property-full
ones involving
\tilde{\sigma}
and
\tilde{\tau}
end with

\mathsf{P}
. Thus,
robust (
\mathsf{R}
) subset-closed
hyperproperty-preserving (
\mathsf{SCH}
) compilation (
\mathsf{C}
)
is

\mathsf{RSCHC^{\sim}}
,
robust (
\mathsf{R}
) two-relational (
\mathsf{2r}
)
safety-preserving (
\mathsf{S}
) compilation (
\mathsf{C}
)
is
\mathsf{R2rSC^{\sim}}
,
etc.

\mathsf{RTC^{\sim}}

5.2
Instance of Trace-Relating Robust Preservation of Trace Properties

This subsection illustrates trace-relating secure compilation when the target
language has strictly more events than the source that target contexts can
exploit to break security.

Source and Target Languages.

The source and target languages used here
are nearly identical expression languages, borrowing from the
syntax of the source language of
§ 3.3
. Both
languages add
sequencing
of expressions, two kinds of
output
events
, and the expressions that generate them:
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{out_{S}}}}\ \mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{n}}}
and

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{out_{S}}}}\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}
usable in source and target, respectively,
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{out_{T}}}}\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}

usable only in the target,
which is the only difference between source and target.
The extra events in the target model the fact that the target language has
an increased ability to perform certain operations, some of them potentially
dangerous (such as writing to the hard drive), which cannot be performed by the
source language, and against which source-level reasoning can therefore offer no
protection.

Both languages and compilation chains now deal with partial programs, contexts
and linking of those two to produce whole programs.
In this setting, a whole program is the combination of a
main expression

to be evaluated and a set of
function definitions
(with distinct names) that can refer to their
argument symbolically and can be called by the main expression and by
other functions. The set of functions of a whole program is the union of the functions of a partial
program and a context; the latter also contains the main expression.
The extensions of the typing rules and the
operational semantics for whole programs
are unsurprising and therefore elided.
The trace model also follows closely that of
§ 3.3
:
it consists of a list of
regular events

(including the new outputs) terminated by a
result event
.
Finally, a partial program and a context can be linked into a whole program
when their functions satisfy the requirements mentioned above.

Relating Traces.

In the present model, source and target traces differ only in the fact that the
target draws (regular) events from a strictly larger set than the source, i.e.,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\Sigma_{T}}}}\supset\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}
.
A natural relation between source and target traces
essentially maps to a given target trace
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
the source trace that erases
from
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
those events that exist only at the target level.
Let
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}|_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}}
indicate trace
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
filtered to retain only those elements included in alphabet
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}
.
We define the trace relation as:

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\quad\equiv\quad\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}|_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}}.

In the opposite direction, a source trace
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}
is related to many target
ones, as any target-only events can be inserted at any point in

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}
. The induced mappings for
\sim
are:

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\ldotp\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}|_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}}\wedge\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right\};

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}})

\displaystyle=\left\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\forall\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\ldotp\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}|_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\Sigma_{S}}}}}\Rightarrow\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}\right\}.

That is, the target guarantee of a source property is that the target has the
same source-level behavior, sprinkled with arbitrary target-level behavior.
Conversely, the source-level obligation of a target property is the aggregate of
those source traces all of whose target-level enrichments are in the target
property.

Since
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{R^{S}}}}
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{R^{T}}}}
are very similar, it is simple to prove that the identity compiler (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\cdot}}}\right\downarrow}}
) from
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{R^{S}}}}
to
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{R^{T}}}}
is secure according to the trace relation
\sim
defined above.

Theorem 5.4
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\cdot}}}\right\downarrow}}
is Secure

)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\cdot}}}\right\downarrow}}
is
\mathsf{RTC^{\sim}}
.

5.3
Instances of Trace-Relating Robust Preservation of Safety and Hypersafety

To provide examples of cross-language trace-relations that preserve safety and hypersafety properties, we show how existing secure compilation results can be interpreted in our framework.
This indicates how the more general theory developed here can already be instantiated to encompass existing results, and that existing proof techniques can be used in order to achieve the secure compilation criteria we define.

For the preservation of safety,
Patrignani and Garg
[
53
]
study a compiler from a typed, concurrent
while
language to an untyped, concurrent
while
language with support for memory capabilities.
As in
§ 3.3
, their source has
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{bool}}}
s and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{nat}}}
s while their target only has
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{nat}}}
s.
Additionally, their source has an ML-like memory (where the domain is locations
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\ell}}}
) while their target has an assembly-like memory (where the domain is natural numbers
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{n}}}
).
Their traces consider context-program interactions and as such they are concatenations of call and return actions with parameters, which can include booleans as well as locations.
Because of the aforementioned differences, they need a cross-language relation to relate source and target actions.

Besides defining a relation on traces (i.e., an instance of
\sim
), they also define a relation between source and target safety properties.
They provide an instantiation of
\tau
that maps all safe source traces to the related target ones.
This ensures that no additional target trace is introduced in the target property, and source safety properties are mapped to target safety ones by
\tau
.
Their compiler is then proven to generate code that respects
\tau
, so they achieve a variation of
\mathsf{RTP^{\mathit{Safe}\circ\tilde{\tau}}}
.

Concerning the preservation of hypersafety,
Patrignani and Garg
[
52
]
consider compilers in a reactive setting where traces are sequences of input (
\alpha?
) and output (
\alpha!
) actions.
In their setting, traces are different between source and target, so they define a cross-language relation on actions that is total on the source actions and injective.
Additionally, their set of target output actions is strictly larger than the source one, as it includes a special action
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
, which is how compiled code must respond to invalid target inputs (i.e., receiving a
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{bool}}}
when a
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{nat}}}
was expected).
Starting from the relation on actions, they define
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
, which is an instance of what we call
\tau
.
Informally, given a set of source traces,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
generates all target traces that are related (pointwise) to a source trace.
Additionally, it generates all traces with interleavings of undesired inputs
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}
followed by
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
as long as removing
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
leaves a trace that relates to the source trace.

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
preserves hypersafety across languages, i.e., it is an instance of
\mathsf{RSCHP^{\mathit{HSafe}\circ\tilde{\tau}}}
mapping source hypersafety to target hypersafety (and safety to safety).

6
Related Work

We already discussed how our results relate to some existing work in
correct compilation
[
34
,
64
]
and secure
compilation
[
2
,
52
,
53
]
.
We also already mentioned that most of our definitions and results
make no assumptions about the structure of traces.
One result that relies on the structure of traces is

Theorem 5.2
, which involves some
finite prefix

m
,
suggesting traces should be some sort of sequences of events (or states),
as customary when one wants to refer to safety
properties
[
14
]
. It is however sufficient to fix
a topology on properties where safety properties coincide with
closed sets
[
48
]
.
Even for reasoning about safety, hypersafety, or arbitrary
hyperproperties, traces can therefore be values, sequences of program
states, or of input output events, or even the recently proposed

interaction trees

[
68
]
. In the latter case
we believe that the compilation from IMP to ASM proposed by

Xia et al.
[
68
]
can be seen as an instance of
\mathsf{HC^{\sim}}
, for
the relation they call “trace equivalence.”

Compilers Where Our Work Could Be Useful.

Our work should be broadly applicable to understanding the guarantees provided
by many verified compilers.
For instance,
Wang et al.
[
67
]
recently proposed a CompCert variant that
compiles all the way down to machine code, and it would be interesting to see if
the model at the end of
§ 3.1
applies there too.
This and many other verified compilers
[
61
,
30
,
12
,
43
]
beyond CakeML
[
64
]
deal with
resource exhaustion and it would be interesting to also apply the ideas of

§ 3.2
to them.

Hur and Dreyer
[
27
]
devised a correct compiler from an ML language to assembly using
a cross-language logical relation to state their
\mathsf{CC}
theorem.
They do not have traces, though were one to add them, the logical relation on
values would serve as the basis for the trace relation and therefore their
result would attain
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.

Switching to more informative traces capturing the interaction between the
program and the context is often used as a proof technique for secure
compilation
[
28
,
51
,
2
]
.
Most of these results consider a cross-language relation, so they probably could
be proved to attain one of the criteria from

Figure 2
.

Generalizations of Compiler Correctness.

The compiler correctness definition of
Morris
[
42
]
was already general
enough to account for trace relations, since it considered a translation between
the semantics of the source program and that of the compiled program, which
he called “decode” in his diagram, reproduced in
Figure 3
(left).
And even some of the more recent compiler correctness definitions preserve this
kind of flexibility
[
55
]
.
While
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
can be seen as an instance of
a definition by
Morris
[
42
]
, we are not aware of any prior work
that investigated the preservation of properties
when the “decode translation” is neither the identity nor a bijection,
and source properties need to be re-interpreted as target ones and vice versa.

source language
target language
target meanings
source meanings
compile
target semantics
decode
source semantics

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\right\downarrow}}
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Z}}}
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{Z}}}^{\#}
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{T}}}
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{S}}}

:
Morris
’s
[
42
]
(left) and
Melton et al.
’s
[
39
]
and
Sabry and Wadler
’s
[

]
(right)

Correct Compilation and Galois Connections.

Melton et al.
[
39
]
and
Sabry and Wadler
[
59
]
expressed a strong
variant of compiler correctness using the diagram of
Figure 3
(right)

[
59
,
39
]
.
They require that compiled programs
parallel
the computation
steps of the original source programs, which can be proven showing
the existence of a
decompilation
map
\#
that makes the
diagram commute, or equivalently, the existence of an adjoint for

\downarrow
(
W\leq W^{\prime}\iff W\twoheadrightarrow W^{\prime}
for both source and target).
The “parallel” intuition can be formalized as an instance of
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
.
Take source and target traces to be finite or infinite sequences of
program states (maximal trace semantics

[
15
]
), and relate them exactly like

Melton et al.
[
39
]
and
Sabry and Wadler
[
59
]
; more details can
be found in
§ 0.C
.

Translation Validation.

Translation validation is an important alternative to proving that all runs of a
compiler are correct.
A variant of
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
for translation validation can simply be obtained by
specializing the definition to a particular
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}
, and one can obtain again
the same trinitarian view.
Similarly for our other criteria, including our extensions of the secure
compilation criteria of
Abate et al.
[
2
]
, which
Busi et al.
[
10
]
seem to
already be considering in the context of translation validation.

7
Conclusion and Future Work

We have extended the property preservation view on compiler correctness to arbitrary
trace relations, and believe that this will be useful for understanding
the guarantees various compilers provide.
An open question is whether, given a compiler, there exists a most precise

\sim
relation for which this compiler is correct.
As mentioned in
§ 1
, every compiler is
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
for some

\sim
, but under which conditions is there a most precise relation?
In practice, more precision may not always be better though, as it may be at
odds with compiler efficiency and may not align with more subjective notions of
usefulness, leading to tradeoffs in the selection of suitable relations.
Finally, another interesting direction for future work is studying whether using
the relation to Galois connections allows to more easily compose trace
relations for different purposes, say, for a compiler whose target language has
undefined behavior, resource exhaustion, and side-channels.
In particular, are there ways to obtain complex relations by combining
simpler ones in a way that eases the compiler verification burden?

Lemma 3
(Special relations and consequences on the adjoints)

Let
X,Y
be two arbitrary sets and
\sim\subseteq X\times Y
.
Assume
\sim
is a total and surjective map from
Y
to
X
. Let

\alpha\leftrightarrows\gamma
be its existential and universal image, i.e.

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\lambda~{}\pi_{X}.~{}\left\{y\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists x\in\pi_{X}.~{}x\sim y\right\}

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\lambda~{}\pi_{Y}.~{}\left\{x\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\forall y.~{}x\sim y\Rightarrow y\in\pi_{Y}\right\}

Then
\tilde{\gamma}=\lambda~{}\pi_{Y}.~{}\left\{x\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists y\in\pi_{Y}.~{}x\sim y\right\}
, and
\tilde{\gamma}
is injective.

\displaystyle\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[~{}\text{definition of}~{}\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}]

\displaystyle g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}\circ\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}\circ\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}~{}\text{injective}]

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{i}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{i}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}}~{}i=1,2]

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W{\models}}}}\mathit{ANI}_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}}^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}}]

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{i}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}}=f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{i}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})~{}i=1,2]

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[~{}\text{functionality of}~{}g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}]

\displaystyle g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}\circ\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})=g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}\circ f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[~{}\text{definition of}~{}\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}]

\displaystyle\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}})=\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}}}}),

so that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\left.\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\right\downarrow}}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\models}}}}\mathit{ANI}_{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}}
.

We now show that if
\mathrel{\mathop{\sim}\limits^{\vbox to-0.5pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{\tiny$\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}$}\vss}}}
is surjective, i.e.,
g^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\bullet}}
injective,

\mathit{ANI}_{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}}\subseteq\mathit{Cl}_{\subseteq}\circ\tilde{\tau}(\mathit{ANI}_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}}^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}})
.

Let
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}\in\mathit{ANI}_{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi^{\#}}}}}^{\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\rho^{\#}}}}}
, we show that

\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}\subseteq\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})
for some

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathit{ANI}_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}}^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}}
.

The source property

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}=\left\{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\right\}=f(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}})

is such that
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}\subseteq\tilde{\tau}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}})
. We only
need to show
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\in\mathit{ANI}_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}}^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\rho}}}}
. Let

\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}},\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{1}}}}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s_{2}}}}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle[\text{by}~{}f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}}~{}\text{for some}~{}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\in\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\pi_{\mathrm{T}}}}}]

\displaystyle\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}}))=\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\phi}}}(f^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}}))\Rightarrow

By
\mathsf{CC^{\sim}}
there exists

\displaystyle\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})=\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\phi}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}^{\scaleobj{0.7}{\circ}}}}})\Rightarrow

(See theorem extra_target_RTCt in
MoreTargetEventsExample.v
, mechanizing a slightly simplified model.)
By definition of
\mathsf{RTC^{\sim}}
we need to find a source context and source trace given a source program, target context and target trace related by compilation and program semantics:
This instantiation is simple since the trace relation is a
function
from
target traces to source traces, and it is easy to clean target contexts to
produce equivalent source context without target-only events.
The proof is a trivial instance of
precise, context-based backtranslation

[
2
,
54
,
47
,
62
]
,
aided by a few straightforward lemmas and where the case of function calls is
guaranteed to terminate by the language.
