t}}}\Rightarrow\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}};\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{W}}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\bm{\rightsquigarrow}}}}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}

where
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}};\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\iff\exists\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{w}}}\in\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{Trace_{I}}}}.~{}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{w}}}\wedge\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{w}}}\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
.

It follows that target guarantees, as well as source obligations, can
be established like in
Section

2.2
, using the composition of
the two relation
\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}};\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}
,
or just by composition of the property mappings. Similarly for

\downarrow^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}}_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}}\in\mathsf{RTC^{\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}}}}

and

\downarrow^{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}\in\mathsf{RTC^{\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}}}
,
their composition

\downarrow^{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}}\in\mathsf{RTC^{(\sim_{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}},\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}}};\sim_{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0.5}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,1,0.5}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0}{0.50}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0}{0.50}{0}{i}}},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}})}}
.

Appendix 0.F
In-Depth Trace-Relating Secure Compilation

We expand
§ 5.3
and delve more in-depth on how to interpret related work as instance of our framework.

0.F.1
Trace-Relating Robust Preservation of Safety Properties

I/O events are not the only instance of events that compilers consider.

Especially in the setting of secure compilation, where a compartmentalized partial program interacts with a context,
interaction traces
are often used
[
49
,
53
,
2
,
29
]
.
Consider a language analogous to that of the previous section, where the context
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{C}}}
defines a set of functions
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{F_{c}}}}
and the program defines a different set
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{F_{p}}}}
.
Interaction traces (generally) record the control flow of calls between these two sets via actions that are
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{call~{}f~{}v}}}
and
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{ret~{}v}}}

[
28
]
.
These actions indicate a call to function
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{f}}}
with parameter
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{v}}}
and a return with return value
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{v}}}
.
In case the context calls a function in
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{F_{p}}}}
(or returns to a function in
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{F_{p}}}}
), the action is decorated with a
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{?}}}
(i.e., those actions are
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{call~{}f~{}v?}}}
and
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{ret~{}v?}}}
).
Dually, the program calling a function in
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{F_{c}}}}
(or returning to it) generates an action decorated with a
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{!}}}
(i.e., those actions are
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{call~{}f~{}v!}}}
and
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{ret~{}v!}}}
).

Patrignani and Garg
[
53
]
consider precisely such a setting.
Moreover, they define a compiler that preserves safety properties of source programs (i.e., it is
\mathit{RSC}
in the sense of
Figure 2
) by relying on capability machines.
The interesting point, however, is that they also consider source and target traces to be distinct since the source has
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{bools}}}
and
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{nats}}}
and the target only has
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{nats}}}
.
10
10
10
Technically, their difference on values also encompasses heap locations and capabilities, but we elide that here since this is sufficient to draw our analogy.

Thus, to prove
\mathit{RSC}
, they rely on a cross-language relation on values, which is scaled to trace actions, and then scaled point-wise to traces (analogously to what we have done in
Sections

3.3
,
3.4
and
5.2
).

Besides defining a relation on traces (which is an instance of
\sim
), they also define a relation between source and target safety properties that supports concurrent programs.
11
11
11
They call those safety properties monitors since they focus on safety
[
60
]
and indicate
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}
with
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{M}}}
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
with
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{M}}}
.

Thus, they really provide an instantiation of
\tau

that maps all safe source traces to the related target ones.
This ensures that no additional target trace is introduced in the target property, and source safety property are mapped to target safety ones by
\tau
.

Their compiler is then proven to generate code that respects
\tau
, so they achieve a variation of
\mathit{RSC}^{\tau}

Their proofs are based on standard techniques either for secure compilation (i.e., trace-based backtranslation
[
54
]
) and for correct compilation (i.e., forward/backward simulation
[
35
]
).

0.F.2
Trace-Relating Robust Preservation of Hypersafety Properties

Patrignani and Garg
[
52
]
study the preservation of hypersafety
from the perspective of secure compilation. Again,
their result can be interpreted in our setting. They consider reactive systems, where trace alphabets are partitioned in input actions
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\alpha?}}}
and output actions
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\alpha!}}}
, whose concatenation generate traces
\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\overline{\alpha?\alpha!}}}}
. We use the same notation as before and indicate such sequences as
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
respectively.
The set of target output actions
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha!}}}
includes an action
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
that has no source counterpart (i.e.,
\nexists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\alpha?}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
), and whose output does not depend on internal state (thus it cannot leak secrets).
12
12
12
Technically, they assume a set of
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
actions, but for this analogy a single action suffices.

By emitting
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
whenever undesired inputs are fed to a compiled program (e.g., passing a
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{nat}}}
when a
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{bool}}}
is expected),
hypersafety is preserved (as
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}
does not leak secrets)
[
52
]
.

More formally, they assume a relation on actions
\sim
that is total on the source actions and injective.
From there, they define
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
—which here corresponds to an instance of
\tau
—that maps the set of valid source traces to the set of valid target traces (that now mention
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
) as follows:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right)}}}=

\displaystyle\ \left\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\in\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{int_{n}}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right)}}}\right\}\;\text{ where}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{int_{0}}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right)}}}=\ \left\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,\exists\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\in\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\wedge\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{s}}}\sim\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\right\}

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{int_{n+1}}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right)}}}=

\displaystyle\ \left\{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\,\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\,(i)\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}\equiv\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}_{1}}}}\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{2}}}}\wedge(ii)\ \mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}_{2}}}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{int_{n}}\left(\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}\right)}}}\wedge(iii)\ {{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{undesired}\left(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}\right)}}}\right\}

where
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0,0}\pgfsys@color@gray@stroke{0}\pgfsys@color@gray@fill{0}{\mathtt{undesired}\left(\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}\right)}}}
indicates that
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}
is an undesired input (intuitively, this is an information that can be derived from the set of source traces
[
52
]
).

Informally, given a set of source traces
\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{0,0.5,1}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{1}{0.50}{0}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{1}{0.50}{0}{0}{\pi_{S}}}}
,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
generates all target traces that are related (pointwise) to a source trace (case
\mathtt{int_{0}}
).
Then (case
\mathtt{int_{n+1}}
), it adds all traces (
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t}}}
) with interleavings of undesired input
\bm{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\alpha?}}}
(point iii) followed by
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{\surd}}}}}}
(point i) as long as the interleavings split a trace
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{t_{1}t_{2}}}}
that has already been mapped (point ii).

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\definecolor[named]{pgfstrokecolor}{rgb}{1,0.23,0.13}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@stroke{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}\pgfsys@color@cmyk@fill{0}{0.77}{0.87}{0}{TPC}}}
is an instance of
\tau
that maps source hypersafety to target hypersafety (and therefore, safety to safety),
thus our theory can be instantiated for the preservation of these classes of hyperproperties as well.
